12:description: When iterative codex review (or similar adversarial reviewer) keeps surfacing finer-grained concerns on the same logical surface, decide disposition by confidence trend AND severity gate. Monotone-decline + low/medium severity → ACCEPT-WITH-DOC. Escalation OR unresolved high/critical → REVERT-AND-REMOVE. Owner-accepted high/critical → MANUAL-OVERRIDE (distinct, tracked). Stop the arc at round 5 regardless.
18:# Source: parent-port with claude-sota path/status adaptations from `Z:/claude/rules/closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` @ parent-ccc `feeefdfacb05d83b2ec77fbb4c2da2fb9ca41f5b` [VERIFIED 2026-05-02]. The confidence-trend disposition AND severity gate are parent-derived (mirror at L31-44); claude-sota adaptations are local surface paths/frontmatter and compressed parent cycle-N evidence as TIER-3 provenance, not claude-sota design authority.
38:**Critical**: stopping iteration is independent of shipping disposition. Hitting round 5 does NOT mean the surface is safe to ship — it means you've spent enough effort on layer-fix and need to switch to a terminal disposition (ACCEPT-WITH-DOC, REVERT-AND-REMOVE, or escalate to user). The terminal disposition has its OWN gates beyond iteration count.
40:## Disposition signal: confidence trend AND severity gate
42:Track codex confidence values across the arc. Disposition is determined by BOTH the trend AND a severity gate (iteration ceiling alone cannot bless unresolved material findings).
44:### Severity gate (mandatory before ACCEPT-WITH-DOC)
46:ACCEPT-WITH-DOC is a **hard deny** for any unresolved `high` or `critical` codex severity. No combination of confidence trend, iteration count, or owner signoff can convert an unresolved high/critical finding into ACCEPT-WITH-DOC. (If you want to ship anyway, see the separate MANUAL-OVERRIDE disposition below.)
48:For findings at `medium` or `low` severity, ACCEPT-WITH-DOC additionally requires AT LEAST ONE of:
53:If neither holds (even at medium severity) → REVERT-AND-REMOVE or kick back to fix-iteration regardless of confidence trend. The severity gate is non-negotiable — iteration count and confidence trend alone cannot bless unresolved material findings.
55:### Outcome A — ACCEPT-WITH-DOC (monotone-decline AND severity-gate satisfied)
57:Confidence declines monotonically across rounds AND the severity gate passes. Codex is acknowledging your fixes address the concern progressively AND the residual risk is verifiably bounded (or low-severity, or owner-accepted).
68:**Anti-example (would FAIL the severity gate)**: round 1 high (0.88) → round 2 high (0.80) → round 3 high (0.73) → "stop at round 5 ceiling, monotone-decline, ACCEPT". This would be wrong — severity stayed high; the underlying defect is still material; ACCEPT-WITH-DOC normalizes shipping known security/correctness regressions.
70:### Outcome B — REVERT-AND-REMOVE (escalation OR unresolved high-severity)
82:### Outcome C — MANUAL-OVERRIDE (owner accepts unresolved high/critical risk)
84:When ACCEPT-WITH-DOC's hard-deny on high/critical blocks shipping but the user or named risk-owner explicitly wants the work to land anyway, this is a SEPARATE disposition — not ACCEPT. Future readers must be able to grep for "we shipped a known high/critical defect with explicit acceptance" without that being conflated with "we did our due diligence and bounded the residual risk."
86:Disposition: ship with a `MANUAL-OVERRIDE` marker in the patch site comment AND in the commit/PR body, citing severity, the specific codex finding, and the owner identity. The marker `MANUAL-OVERRIDE` (case-sensitive) signals that NO automated process closed the loop — a human accepted material risk explicitly. Use of this disposition should be rare; track each instance as a code-review-burn metric.
88:The separation matters because conflating MANUAL-OVERRIDE with ACCEPT lets future commits silently bless known material defects.
96:- **Burn the closed-loop discipline**: codex's value comes from being adversarial; if you keep ACCEPT'ing without checking the confidence trend, you train yourself to ignore real findings
111:2. **REVERT-AND-REMOVE** if: confidence escalates between rounds (any round count), OR last-round severity is `high`/`critical` and you have no MANUAL-OVERRIDE
112:3. **ACCEPT-WITH-DOC** ONLY if: last-round severity is `medium` or lower AND severity gate passes (concrete verification OR severity downgrade in evidence) AND confidence declined monotonically. Hard deny for any unresolved `high`/`critical`.
113:4. **MANUAL-OVERRIDE** if: user/owner explicitly accepts unresolved high/critical risk for this specific commit. Distinct from ACCEPT — uses `MANUAL-OVERRIDE` marker, tracked as code-review-burn metric.
114:5. **Escalate to user** if: round ≥ 5 with high-severity unresolved finding, no clear REVERT path (e.g., feature has callers we can't easily migrate), and no owner available for MANUAL-OVERRIDE.
116:## Anti-pattern: ACCEPT-WITH-DOC on escalating concern
120:Lesson: ACCEPT-WITH-DOC is only valid when codex's confidence trend confirms convergence. Watch the trend, not just the current round.
124:Outcome B (REVERT-AND-REMOVE) is not necessarily terminal for the underlying work. A common recovery shape is: REVERT removes the over-broad surface → a later, narrower fix-forward re-introduces the work with the invariant preserved + scope bounded → that narrower fix-forward converges via Outcome A (monotone-decline ACCEPT). Track the two as separate arcs (the REVERT closes arc 1; the narrower re-introduction opens arc 2) rather than as a single iteration.
131:- `.claude/rules/evidence-policy.md` — claim-marking discipline; ACCEPT-WITH-DOC ship comments should use `[VERIFIED]` or `[VERIFIED yyyy-mm-dd]` markers per evidence-policy.

2:Wave 62 fire 8 - sibling cite-import per CR-12 TERTIARY (HONEST-NON-FINDING-gated)
12:description: At the team-lead synthesis-pre-commit gate, verify every teammate ship-report claim against `git diff` (claimed edits) and `git status` (claimed new files), with smoke-import for new code, BEFORE trusting the report. Recovery is invariant under failure-mode (over-report / under-report / proxy-restart / 429 / network blip / honest-non-finding). Within-cycle, verdict-flips on a verified blocker must decompose the flip into per-claim evidence — a single fact-confirm doesn't authorize multi-claim flip.
24:Ship-reports drift from git reality in BOTH directions OR fail to surface a finding entirely (HONEST-NON-FINDING — see §Reporting categories), and the failure-mode taxonomy keeps growing (agent flake, proxy restart, 429 retries, Windows loopback transients, model-side stalls, infrastructure deploys mid-cycle). The synthesis-layer recovery is the same single check across all of them: `git diff` + `git status` + smoke-import. Without this check, false-failure re-spawns waste 2-5 min each; false-success commits introduce silent no-ops into git history.
42:## SubagentStop transcript-mining (4th OVER/UNDER/HNF detection axis — iter-109 closure)
49:- **OVER (transcript-vs-report)**: agent's `verdict_one_line` claims `DONE: 5 fixes` but transcript shows `tool_count=2` — transcript truth refutes ship-report. Recovery: investigate via JSONL trace + revisit ship; same shape as `git diff` OVER.
50:- **UNDER (transcript surfaces unreported tool-errors)**: agent's report doesn't mention errors but transcript shows `tool_errors > 0` — agent encountered failures it suppressed. Recovery: surface the suppressed errors per cardinal-rule 7 (CLAUDE.md §Hard Rules, bullet "REPORT errors before routing around them"); pivot ship if errors load-bearing.
51:- **HONEST-NON-FINDING (transcript empty / parse failure)**: `parse_status != "ok"` — transcript missing or unparseable. Recovery: HNF disposition; do NOT re-spawn agent on the assumption transcript exists; treat axis as closed-without-finding.
61:## Reporting categories — OVER, UNDER, HONEST-NON-FINDING
67:| **OVER** | Report claims more than git shows (claimed edit reverted; claimed file absent) | `git reflog` + infrastructure event log; investigate before re-spawn or commit |
68:| **UNDER** | Report claims less than git shows (claimed nothing, file landed; partial credit) | `git diff` to surface the orphan change; accept work + correct report → commit |
69:| **HONEST-NON-FINDING** | Report says "I probed for X, found nothing" — when the brief assumed X exists | Pivot ship — DROP / REDIRECT / amend brief. The HNF IS the deliverable; do NOT re-spawn the agent to "find X anyway" |
71:**Why HNF matters**: it's the highest-value report shape but easiest to mistake for "agent failed to ship." An agent that probes the brief's assumption and finds it wrong has saved a wrong-direction ship — that's a SUCCESS at the discipline layer, not a failure. The synthesis-layer recovery is to update the brief / drop the patch / redirect to the correct target — never to re-spawn under the same (now-refuted) assumption.
73:**HNF as cite-vs-concept sibling**: HNF reports are often the empirical refutation of a brief-level cite-vs-concept failure. Brief asserts X exists at file:line; agent probes; X doesn't exist. Brief's assumption was a CONCEPT (loose familiarity) not a CITE (verified file:line). HNF surfaces this gap. (Cite-vs-concept semantic-equivalence remains FORWARD-REF in claude-sota; the parent CCC narrative is TIER-3 provenance only until ported with TIER-1/TIER-2 backing.)
81:| **UPSTREAM-CLAIM** | About external project's state (README content, repo metadata, framing) — subject to upstream evolution between verdict-cycle and probe-cycle | HIGH refutation candidate — upstream may have reformed |
82:| **OPERATIONAL-CLAIM** | About OUR harness state (live MCP invocations, hook-fire counts, integration depth) — controlled by us, slow-moving | LOW refutation likelihood — state changes only when we change it |
83:| **CATEGORY-CLAIM** | About semantic mismatch between candidate and our use-case (RAG vs session-memory, batch vs streaming) — requires upstream pivot to flip | LOW refutation likelihood — categorical signals stable |
92:**Distinguishes from §Reporting categories above**: §Reporting categories classifies REPORT shape (OVER/UNDER/HNF — about agent's output). §Subclaim-type discriminator classifies SOURCE shape (UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY — about what's being verified). Orthogonal axes — a UPSTREAM-CLAIM probe may produce HNF, OVER, or UNDER report.
103:**Probe rule**: run the strongest available compile/schema/validation check first. If it passes, downgrade only **shape-refutation budget**; still run semantic/runtime probes for side effects, credentials, provider limitations, type coercion, `any`/untyped fields, unsupported schema keywords, and producer/consumer boundary mismatches. **Compile-pass is NOT deploy-pass.**
155:- §Reporting categories (OVER/UNDER/HONEST-NON-FINDING) — covers verification-output classification AFTER spec is consumed; this section covers spec-input quality BEFORE consumption
160:- **File looks present but doesn't compile**: a common UNDER-report failure mode. A `Write` can land successfully (size > 0) yet content has a syntax error from partial render. ALWAYS pair `git status` (presence) with smoke-import (`python -c "import X"`) for new Python files.

12:description: When iterative codex review (or similar adversarial reviewer) keeps surfacing finer-grained concerns on the same logical surface, decide disposition by confidence trend AND severity gate. Monotone-decline + low/medium severity → ACCEPT-WITH-DOC. Escalation OR unresolved high/critical → REVERT-AND-REMOVE. Owner-accepted high/critical → MANUAL-OVERRIDE (distinct, tracked). Stop the arc at round 5 regardless.
22:## Pattern signature
30:## Stop criteria — process decision only
38:**Critical**: stopping iteration is independent of shipping disposition. Hitting round 5 does NOT mean the surface is safe to ship — it means you've spent enough effort on layer-fix and need to switch to a terminal disposition (ACCEPT-WITH-DOC, REVERT-AND-REMOVE, or escalate to user). The terminal disposition has its OWN gates beyond iteration count.
40:## Disposition signal: confidence trend AND severity gate
44:### Severity gate (mandatory before ACCEPT-WITH-DOC)
46:ACCEPT-WITH-DOC is a **hard deny** for any unresolved `high` or `critical` codex severity. No combination of confidence trend, iteration count, or owner signoff can convert an unresolved high/critical finding into ACCEPT-WITH-DOC. (If you want to ship anyway, see the separate MANUAL-OVERRIDE disposition below.)
48:For findings at `medium` or `low` severity, ACCEPT-WITH-DOC additionally requires AT LEAST ONE of:
53:If neither holds (even at medium severity) → REVERT-AND-REMOVE or kick back to fix-iteration regardless of confidence trend. The severity gate is non-negotiable — iteration count and confidence trend alone cannot bless unresolved material findings.
55:### Outcome A — ACCEPT-WITH-DOC (monotone-decline AND severity-gate satisfied)
66:Disposition: ship a doc-comment ACCEPT in the patch site explaining threat model + verification reference + ROI + upstream issue queue. Future workers see the explicit acceptance; arc closes cleanly.
68:**Anti-example (would FAIL the severity gate)**: round 1 high (0.88) → round 2 high (0.80) → round 3 high (0.73) → "stop at round 5 ceiling, monotone-decline, ACCEPT". This would be wrong — severity stayed high; the underlying defect is still material; ACCEPT-WITH-DOC normalizes shipping known security/correctness regressions.
70:### Outcome B — REVERT-AND-REMOVE (escalation OR unresolved high-severity)
80:Disposition: REMOVE the surface entirely. The smallest LOC change that closes the security/correctness gap is feature deletion, not yet-another-patch. Re-add gated on proper fix later.
82:### Outcome C — MANUAL-OVERRIDE (owner accepts unresolved high/critical risk)
84:When ACCEPT-WITH-DOC's hard-deny on high/critical blocks shipping but the user or named risk-owner explicitly wants the work to land anyway, this is a SEPARATE disposition — not ACCEPT. Future readers must be able to grep for "we shipped a known high/critical defect with explicit acceptance" without that being conflated with "we did our due diligence and bounded the residual risk."
86:Disposition: ship with a `MANUAL-OVERRIDE` marker in the patch site comment AND in the commit/PR body, citing severity, the specific codex finding, and the owner identity. The marker `MANUAL-OVERRIDE` (case-sensitive) signals that NO automated process closed the loop — a human accepted material risk explicitly. Use of this disposition should be rare; track each instance as a code-review-burn metric.
88:The separation matters because conflating MANUAL-OVERRIDE with ACCEPT lets future commits silently bless known material defects.
90:## Why this matters
98:## Application checklist
111:2. **REVERT-AND-REMOVE** if: confidence escalates between rounds (any round count), OR last-round severity is `high`/`critical` and you have no MANUAL-OVERRIDE
112:3. **ACCEPT-WITH-DOC** ONLY if: last-round severity is `medium` or lower AND severity gate passes (concrete verification OR severity downgrade in evidence) AND confidence declined monotonically. Hard deny for any unresolved `high`/`critical`.
113:4. **MANUAL-OVERRIDE** if: user/owner explicitly accepts unresolved high/critical risk for this specific commit. Distinct from ACCEPT — uses `MANUAL-OVERRIDE` marker, tracked as code-review-burn metric.
114:5. **Escalate to user** if: round ≥ 5 with high-severity unresolved finding, no clear REVERT path (e.g., feature has callers we can't easily migrate), and no owner available for MANUAL-OVERRIDE.
116:## Anti-pattern: ACCEPT-WITH-DOC on escalating concern
120:Lesson: ACCEPT-WITH-DOC is only valid when codex's confidence trend confirms convergence. Watch the trend, not just the current round.
122:## REVERT-then-recovery trajectory
124:Outcome B (REVERT-AND-REMOVE) is not necessarily terminal for the underlying work. A common recovery shape is: REVERT removes the over-broad surface → a later, narrower fix-forward re-introduces the work with the invariant preserved + scope bounded → that narrower fix-forward converges via Outcome A (monotone-decline ACCEPT). Track the two as separate arcs (the REVERT closes arc 1; the narrower re-introduction opens arc 2) rather than as a single iteration.

22:## Why
24:Ship-reports drift from git reality in BOTH directions OR fail to surface a finding entirely (HONEST-NON-FINDING — see §Reporting categories), and the failure-mode taxonomy keeps growing (agent flake, proxy restart, 429 retries, Windows loopback transients, model-side stalls, infrastructure deploys mid-cycle). The synthesis-layer recovery is the same single check across all of them: `git diff` + `git status` + smoke-import. Without this check, false-failure re-spawns waste 2-5 min each; false-success commits introduce silent no-ops into git history.

42:## SubagentStop transcript-mining (4th OVER/UNDER/HNF detection axis — iter-109 closure)
51:- **HONEST-NON-FINDING (transcript empty / parse failure)**: `parse_status != "ok"` — transcript missing or unparseable. Recovery: HNF disposition; do NOT re-spawn agent on the assumption transcript exists; treat axis as closed-without-finding.
61:## Reporting categories — OVER, UNDER, HONEST-NON-FINDING
69:| **HONEST-NON-FINDING** | Report says "I probed for X, found nothing" — when the brief assumed X exists | Pivot ship — DROP / REDIRECT / amend brief. The HNF IS the deliverable; do NOT re-spawn the agent to "find X anyway" |
71:**Why HNF matters**: it's the highest-value report shape but easiest to mistake for "agent failed to ship." An agent that probes the brief's assumption and finds it wrong has saved a wrong-direction ship — that's a SUCCESS at the discipline layer, not a failure. The synthesis-layer recovery is to update the brief / drop the patch / redirect to the correct target — never to re-spawn under the same (now-refuted) assumption.
73:**HNF as cite-vs-concept sibling**: HNF reports are often the empirical refutation of a brief-level cite-vs-concept failure. Brief asserts X exists at file:line; agent probes; X doesn't exist. Brief's assumption was a CONCEPT (loose familiarity) not a CITE (verified file:line). HNF surfaces this gap. (Cite-vs-concept semantic-equivalence remains FORWARD-REF in claude-sota; the parent CCC narrative is TIER-3 provenance only until ported with TIER-1/TIER-2 backing.)
75:## Subclaim-type discriminator (UPSTREAM / OPERATIONAL / CATEGORY)
92:**Distinguishes from §Reporting categories above**: §Reporting categories classifies REPORT shape (OVER/UNDER/HNF — about agent's output). §Subclaim-type discriminator classifies SOURCE shape (UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY — about what's being verified). Orthogonal axes — a UPSTREAM-CLAIM probe may produce HNF, OVER, or UNDER report.

45:When an agent (architect / codex T1 prescription / sota-researcher / Explore / general-purpose / codex-rescue / gpt5-reviewer) returns a ship-prescription with `prescribed_edits` / specific file:line claims about gaps to close, **pre-apply the Mia rule (verify against runtime state) BEFORE writing any Edit**. The agent's prescription IS NOT TRUSTED until verified — even when conf ≥ 0.90 + Pattern A trigger.
47:The pattern: **AGENT reads brief context but DOES NOT probe runtime state → produces CATEGORY-CLAIM that LOOKS LIKE OPERATIONAL-CLAIM** (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator`). Agents systematically conflate "should be wired" with "is wired", "should be 80" with "is 80", "is unwired" with "is wired", without checking actual state via `git log` / `Grep` / `Glob`. This rule converts post-hoc OVER detection (existing `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` shape) into PRE-emptive verification (this rule's shape) BEFORE any Edit fires.
49:## When this rule applies
53:- `prescribed_edits` listing specific file:line + content gaps
60:- Pure analysis returns (no `prescribed_edits` / no concrete file:line claim)
62:- Pure read-only review output (gpt5-reviewer audit summary without specific edits)

Do NOT silently apply the prescription; document the refutation in commit body

152:Agents are calibrated to find gaps; they're NOT calibrated to verify "is the gap still open?" before prescribing. Their reasoning surface is BRIEF + LOCAL-CONTEXT-WINDOW, not REPO-RUNTIME-STATE. The Mia-rule pre-apply discipline closes this gap by inserting a cheap (~1-3s) runtime probe between prescription-receipt and Edit-application.
156:The rule is **bidirectional** — saves false-OVER ship cycles (rows 4, 6, 7) AND validates true-positive gap-claims (rows 5, 8). Pattern A admissibility filter shape: drop OVER, apply VERIFIED-GENUINE atomically.
160:- **Trust agent prescription verbatim because conf ≥ 0.90** — refuted by n=3 same-arc evidence + n=5 cross-arc evidence. Codex T1 verdict at conf=0.92 still produced an OVER on Wave 13 prescription #2; architect at no-conf-stated produced 2 OVERs (Wave 12 S1 + S3); Wave 8 sota-researcher at no-conf-stated produced staleness drift on Ship #1 spec. Confidence does NOT correlate with prescription-truth on already-closed surfaces.
163:- **Apply prescriptions in single atomic batch without per-prescription verification** — refuted by Wave 13 same-arc evidence: codex T1 prescription #2 was OVER in a 6-prescription batch; if I'd applied all 6 at once, the duplicate would have shipped. Per-prescription verification is necessary even when the agent batches.
164:- **Conflate OVER with HONEST-NON-FINDING** — these are distinct `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories`: OVER = report claims more than git shows (refuted prescription class); HNF = report says "I probed for X, found nothing" (agent did the probe). A refuted gap-prescription where I probed and found the gap closed = OVER, NOT HNF (the agent didn't probe; I did).
165:- **Apply Mia BEFORE codex T1 finishes** — Mia is a PRE-Edit gate; if codex T1 is still running, wait for verdict (Pattern A) OR honor Pattern B HNF disposition (timeout-without-verdict). DO NOT race Mia probe + Pattern A apply against an in-flight T1 — the T1 may correct or refute the Mia prescription itself (recursive: codex T1 ratifies Mia ratifies prescription).
167:- **PATH-only probe for INSTALL-class prescriptions** — refuted by Wave 112 Ship A1+2CC archeology n=29→36 (5 OVER catches in single ship). `command -v <cli>` only checks PATH-availability — MISSES alternate install paths (`.local/bin/`, `.cargo/bin/`, `~/go/bin/`, npm-global, WinGet, uv tool dirs). Cost: 115 MB shadow installs + 3 follow-up ships to operationally close. Use the fortified multi-channel probe per §Alternate-install-path probe discipline above.

**Rationale**

Outcome A is not formally available. `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` says the disposition signal is **confidence trend AND severity gate**, with Outcome A requiring monotone confidence decline plus satisfied severity gate. This fire has Agent A HNF plus infrastructure-degraded missing confirmations, not a same-surface multi-round confidence trajectory. The checklist is explicit that `ACCEPT-WITH-DOC` requires last-round severity medium-or-lower, concrete verification or severity downgrade, and monotonically declined confidence. See `Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/rules/closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:40-57,111-112 @ HEAD 5f6d51ec4de01396f4fda61710ced78f5c9f2c0a`.

Outcome B is also the wrong semantics. The REPLACE premise is refuted, but the landed W200 documentation/provenance is not itself an unresolved high/critical defect requiring deletion. The HNF rule says when the brief assumed X exists and probing finds nothing, the HNF **is** the deliverable; the recovery is to pivot, drop, redirect, or amend the brief, not re-spawn to find X anyway. That maps to “redirect the goal from REPLACE to AMBER/cite reinforcement,” not “revert the W200 arc evidence.” See `Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md:61-71 @ HEAD 5f6d51ec4de01396f4fda61710ced78f5c9f2c0a`.

Outcome C is not justified. `MANUAL-OVERRIDE` is reserved for explicit owner acceptance of an unresolved material finding, with a marker in patch-site comment and commit/PR body. The current blockers are context pressure and agent-fleet degradation, not an explicitly accepted known high/critical defect. Asking Codex which disposition is correct is not itself owner acceptance. See `Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/rules/closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md:82-88,113-114 @ HEAD 5f6d51ec4de01396f4fda61710ced78f5c9f2c0a`.

The correct rule family is CR-12 + HNF, not CLRN Outcome A/B/C. CR-12 permits cite-import-AMBER only when upstream-parity probing returns HNF evidence showing no upstream parity; its lattice also maps duplicate functionality and cite-class-canonical cases to reject-for-fit or accept-as-cite-reference rather than runtime replacement. See `Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/rules/cardinal-rule-12-upstream-install-priority.md:17-24,31-63 @ HEAD 5f6d51ec4de01396f4fda61710ced78f5c9f2c0a`.
